1.34
),
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}=\left[\boldsymbol{\varphi_{ij}^{*}},\ \psi_{ij}^{*}\right]
:

\displaystyle P_{N}\begin{bmatrix}P_{q}(m\boldsymbol{u})\\
{\mathcal{B}}_{q}Dv\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{M,\ell_{i}}\left[\left\langle P_{q}(m\boldsymbol{u}),\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}_{ij}\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\omega),\boldsymbol{L}^{q^{\prime}}_{\sigma}(\omega)}+\left\langle v,D^{*}{\mathcal{B}}^{*}_{q}\psi_{ij}^{*}\right\rangle_{L^{q}(\Gamma),L^{q^{\prime}}(\Gamma)}\right]\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{ij}

(4.17)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{M,\ell_{i}}\left[\left\langle m\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}_{ij}\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\omega),\boldsymbol{L}^{q^{\prime}}_{\sigma}(\omega)}+\left\langle v,\left.-\frac{\partial\psi_{ij}^{*}}{\partial\nu}\right|_{\Gamma}\right\rangle_{L^{q}(\Gamma),L^{q^{\prime}}(\Gamma)}\right]\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{ij}

(4.18)

and (
4.16
) is verified. In going from (
4.17
) to (
4.18
) we have recalled
\displaystyle D^{*}{\mathcal{B}}^{*}_{q}f=-\frac{\partial f}{\partial\nu},\ f\in{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{B}}^{*}_{q})
, see (
B.11
) of Appendix
B
for
\kappa=1
. Next, we seek an interior control
\boldsymbol{u}
acting on
\omega
and a boundary control
v
acting on
\widetilde{\Gamma}
at first of the form

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mu_{k}(t)\boldsymbol{u}_{k},\ \boldsymbol{u}_{k}\in\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}\subset\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)

(4.19)

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\nu_{k}(t)f_{k}\in{\mathcal{F}}\subset W^{2-{}^{1}\!/_{q},q}(\widetilde{\Gamma})

(4.20)

where

{\mathcal{F}}=\text{span }\left\{\left.\frac{\partial\psi_{ij}^{*}}{\partial\nu}\right|_{\widetilde{\Gamma}},\ i=1,\dots,M;\ j=1,\dots,\ell_{i}\right\}\subset W^{2-{}^{1}\!/_{q},q}(\widetilde{\Gamma}).

(4.21)

Thus substituting (
4.19
), (
4.20
), in (
4.16
) we obtain

P_{N}\begin{bmatrix}P_{q}(m\boldsymbol{u})\\
{\mathcal{B}}_{q}Dv\end{bmatrix}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{M,\ell_{i}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left[\left\langle m\boldsymbol{u}_{k},\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}_{ij}\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\omega),\boldsymbol{L}^{q^{\prime}}_{\sigma}(\omega)}\mu_{k}(t)\right.\\
\left.-\left\langle f_{k},\left.\frac{\partial\psi_{ij}^{*}}{\partial\nu}\right|_{\Gamma}\right\rangle_{L^{q}(\Gamma),L^{q^{\prime}}(\Gamma)}\nu_{k}(t)\right]\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{ij}.

(4.22)

Accordingly, in view of (
4.22
), we can rewrite the
\boldsymbol{w}_{N}
-problem (
3.6a
) as

\text{on }\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}:\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime}_{N}=\mathbb{A}_{q,N}^{u}\boldsymbol{w}_{N}+\sum_{i,j=1}^{M,\ell_{i}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left[\left\langle m\boldsymbol{u}_{k},\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}_{ij}\right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\omega),\boldsymbol{L}^{q^{\prime}}_{\sigma}(\omega)}\mu_{k}(t)\right.\\
-\left.\left\langle f_{k},\left.\frac{\partial\psi_{ij}^{*}}{\partial\nu}\right|_{\Gamma}\right\rangle_{L^{q}(\Gamma),L^{q^{\prime}}(\Gamma)}\nu_{k}(t)\right]\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{ij}.

(4.23)

Eq (
4.23
) is the perfect counterpart of
[
62
, Eq (6.24)]
.

On the basis of expansion (
4.23
), we introduce the
\ell_{i}\times K
matrix
W_{i}
and the
\ell_{i}\times K
matrix
U_{i}
,
i=1,\dots,M

W_{i}=\begin{bmatrix}\left\langle f_{1},\partial_{\nu}\psi^{*}_{i1}|_{\Gamma}\right\rangle_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}&\cdots&\left\langle f_{K},\partial_{\nu}\psi^{*}_{i1}|_{\Gamma}\right\rangle_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}\\
\left\langle f_{1},\partial_{\nu}\psi^{*}_{i2}|_{\Gamma}\right\rangle_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}&\cdots&\left\langle f_{K},\partial_{\nu}\psi^{*}_{i2}|_{\Gamma}\right\rangle_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}\\
\vdots&&\vdots\\
\left\langle f_{1},\partial_{\nu}\psi^{*}_{i\ell_{i}}|_{\Gamma}\right\rangle_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}&\cdots&\left\langle f_{K},\partial_{\nu}\psi^{*}_{i\ell_{i}}|_{\Gamma}\right\rangle_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}\end{bmatrix}:\ \ell_{i}\times K\ \left\langle\ ,\ \right\rangle_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}=\left\langle\ ,\ \right\rangle_{L^{q}(\widetilde{\Gamma}),L^{q^{\prime}}(\widetilde{\Gamma})}

(4.24)

U_{i}=\begin{bmatrix}\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}_{i1}\right\rangle_{\omega}&\cdots&\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{K},\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}_{i1}\right\rangle_{\omega}\\
\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}_{i2}\right\rangle_{\omega}&\cdots&\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{K},\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}_{i2}\right\rangle_{\omega}\\
\vdots&&\vdots\\
\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}_{i\ell_{i}}\right\rangle_{\omega}&\cdots&\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{K},\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}_{i\ell_{i}}\right\rangle_{\omega}\\
\end{bmatrix}:\ \ell_{i}\times K\ \left\langle\ ,\ \right\rangle_{\omega}=\left\langle\ ,\ \right\rangle_{\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\omega)}

(4.25)

W=\left\lVert\left\langle f_{r},\partial_{\nu}\psi^{*}_{ij}|_{\Gamma}\right\rangle_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}\right\rVert=\begin{bmatrix}W_{1}\\
W_{2}\\
\vdots\\
W_{M}\end{bmatrix};\ N\times K,\quad U=\left\lVert\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{r},\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}_{ij}\right\rangle_{\omega}\right\rVert=\begin{bmatrix}U_{1}\\
U_{2}\\
\vdots\\
U_{M}\end{bmatrix}:N\times K.

(4.26)

Next, we represent the
N
-dimensional vector
\boldsymbol{w}_{N}\in\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}
given by (
4.12
) as column vector
\hat{\boldsymbol{w}}=[\boldsymbol{w}_{N}]_{\beta}
, with respect to the basis
\beta
in (
4.13b
). Rewrite (
4.12
) as

\boldsymbol{w}_{N}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{M,\ell_{i}}\boldsymbol{w}^{ij}_{N}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{ij};\ \text{set }\hat{\boldsymbol{w}}=\text{col}\left[\boldsymbol{w}^{11}_{N},\dots,\boldsymbol{w}^{1\ell_{1}}_{N},\dots,\boldsymbol{w}^{i1}_{N},\dots,\boldsymbol{w}^{i\ell_{i}}_{N},\dots,\right.\\
\left.\boldsymbol{w}^{M1}_{N},\dots,\boldsymbol{w}^{M\ell_{M}}_{N}\right]

(4.27)

N=\ell_{1}+\dots+\ell_{M}
. Thus, by (
4.22
)-(
4.27
) we can write

\begin{bmatrix}\displaystyle P_{N}\begin{bmatrix}P_{q}(m\boldsymbol{u})\\
{\mathcal{B}}_{q}Dv\end{bmatrix}\end{bmatrix}_{\beta}=\begin{bmatrix}W_{1}\\
W_{2}\\
\vdots\\
W_{M}\end{bmatrix}\hat{\nu}_{K}+\begin{bmatrix}U_{1}\\
U_{2}\\
\vdots\\
U_{M}\end{bmatrix}\hat{\mu}_{k}:\ N\times 1,\quad\hat{\nu}_{K}=\begin{bmatrix}\nu^{1}_{K}\\
\vdots\\
\nu^{k}_{K}\\
\vdots\\
\nu^{K}_{K}\end{bmatrix},\ \hat{\mu}_{K}=\begin{bmatrix}\mu^{1}_{K}\\
\vdots\\
\mu^{k}_{K}\\
\vdots\\
\mu^{K}_{K}\end{bmatrix}.

(4.28)

Then, in
\mathbb{C}^{N}
, with respect to the basis
\beta
of normalized eigenvectors of
\mathbb{A}_{q,N}^{u}
, we may rewrite system (
4.23
) via (
4.14
) and (
4.28
) as

\hat{\boldsymbol{w}}^{\prime}_{N}=\Lambda\hat{\boldsymbol{w}}_{N}+W\hat{\nu}_{K}+U\hat{\mu}_{K}=\Lambda\hat{\boldsymbol{w}}_{N}+B\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\nu}_{K}\\
\hat{\mu}_{K}\end{bmatrix},\quad B=[W,U]:N\times 2K.

(4.29)

Then, the operator system (
4.23
) on
\displaystyle\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}
has in (
4.29
) its representation in
\mathbb{C}^{N}
, characterized by the pair
\displaystyle\{\Lambda,B\}
, with
\Lambda
the free dynamics matrix and
B
the control matrix. Our next goal is to guarantee that the pair
\displaystyle\{\Lambda,B\}
is controllable.

We then obtain the following counterpart of
[
62
, Theorem 6.1]
.

Theorem 4.1
.

Assume (
4.11
); that is, that
\mathbb{A}_{q,N}^{u}
is semisimple on
\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}
. System (
4.29
) i.e. the pair
\displaystyle\{\Lambda,B\}
is controllable in case

\text{rank }[W_{i},\ U_{i}]=\ell_{i},\ i=1,\dots,M.

(4.30)

In fact, with reference to (
4.19
) and (
4.20
), it is possible to select boundary vectors
f_{1},\dots,f_{K}\in{\mathcal{F}}\subset W^{2-{}^{1}\!/_{q},q}(\widetilde{\Gamma})
with support on
\widetilde{\Gamma}
, and interior vectors
\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{u}_{K}\in\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\omega)
with support on
\omega
such that (
4.30
) holds true, specifically

\text{rank }[W_{i},U_{i}]=\\
\text{rank}\begin{bmatrix}\left\langle f_{1},\partial_{\nu}\psi^{*}_{i1}|_{\Gamma}\right\rangle_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}&\cdots&\left\langle f_{K},\partial_{\nu}\psi^{*}_{i1}|_{\Gamma}\right\rangle_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}&\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}_{i1}\right\rangle_{\omega}&\cdots&\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{K},\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}_{i1}\right\rangle_{\omega}\\
\begin{picture}\put(164.0,22.0){\line{0}{-1}{6.0}}\put(164.0,13.0){\line{0}{-1}{6.0}}\put(164.0,4.0){\line{0}{-1}{6.0}}\put(164.0,-5.0){\line{0}{-1}{6.0}}\put(164.0,-14.0){\line{0}{-1}{6.0}}\put(164.0,-23.0){\line{0}{-1}{6.0}}\put(164.0,-32.0){\line{0}{-1}{6.0}}\end{picture}\left\langle f_{1},\partial_{\nu}\psi^{*}_{i2}|_{\Gamma}\right\rangle_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}&\cdots&\left\langle f_{K},\partial_{\nu}\psi^{*}_{i2}|_{\Gamma}\right\rangle_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}&\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}_{i2}\right\rangle_{\omega}&\cdots&\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{K},\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}_{i2}\right\rangle_{\omega}\\
\vdots&&\vdots&\vdots&&\vdots\\
\left\langle f_{1},\partial_{\nu}\psi^{*}_{i\ell_{i}}|_{\Gamma}\right\rangle_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}&\cdots&\left\langle f_{K},\partial_{\nu}\psi^{*}_{i\ell_{i}}|_{\Gamma}\right\rangle_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}&\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}_{i\ell_{i}}\right\rangle_{\omega}&\cdots&\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{K},\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}_{i\ell_{i}}\right\rangle_{\omega}\end{bmatrix}\\
=\ell_{i},\ i=1,\dots,M.

(4.31)

Proof.

This is the counterpart of the proof
[
61
, Theorem 6.1]
. As in that paper, the present proof rests on a suitable Unique Continuation Property, Theorem
B.1
of Appendix
B
as documented below.

In general. in seeking that the
\ell_{i}
rows (of length
2K
) in matrix (
4.31
) be linearly independent, we see that the full rank statement (
4.31
) will hold true if and only if we can exclude that each of the two sets of vectors

\left\{\partial_{\nu}\psi_{i1}^{*},\dots,\partial_{\nu}\psi_{i\ell_{i}}^{*}\right\}\text{ in }L^{q}(\widetilde{\Gamma})\quad\text{and}\quad\left\{\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{i1}^{*},\dots,\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{i\ell_{i}}^{*}\right\}\text{ in }\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\omega)

(4.32)

are linearly independent with the same linear independence relation in the two cases; that is, if and only if we can establish that
we cannot
have simultaneously

\partial_{\nu}\psi_{i\ell_{i}}^{*}=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell_{i}-1}\alpha_{j}\partial_{\nu}\psi_{ij}^{*}\text{ in }L^{q}(\widetilde{\Gamma})\quad\text{and}\quad\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{i\ell_{i}}^{*}=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell_{i}-1}\alpha_{j}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{ij}^{*}\text{ in }\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\omega)

(4.33)

with the same constants
\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{\ell_{i}-1}
in both expansions
[
61
]
.

Claim: Statement (
4.33
) is false.
By contradiction, suppose that both linear combinations in (
4.33
) hold true. Define the
(d+1)
-vector
\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}=\{\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*},\psi^{*}\}
(depending on
i
) on
\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\times L^{q}(\Omega)
by setting

\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}\equiv\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}\\
\psi^{*}\end{bmatrix}\equiv\sum_{j=1}^{\ell_{i}-1}\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{j}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{ij}^{*}\\
\alpha_{j}\psi_{ij}^{*}\end{bmatrix}-\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{i\ell_{i}}^{*}\\
\psi_{i\ell_{i}}^{*}\end{bmatrix}=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell_{i}-1}\alpha_{j}\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}_{ij}-\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}_{i\ell_{i}},\ i=1,\dots,M;\ q\geq 2.

(4.34)

Thus, in view of (
4.33
), we obtain

\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}\equiv 0\text{ in }\omega,\quad\partial_{\nu}\psi^{*}|_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}\equiv 0.

(4.35)

Moreover, since
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}_{ij}=\{\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}_{ij},\psi^{*}_{ij}\}
is an eigenvector of the operator
\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{q,N}^{*}
corresponding to the (unstable) eigenvalue
\bar{\lambda}_{i}
, then so is
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}:\ \mathbb{A}_{q,N}^{*}\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}=\bar{\lambda}_{i}\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}
. Then
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}=\{\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*},\psi^{*}\}
satisfies the corresponding PDE version of the eigenvector identity as given by (
4.10
), see (
B.4
) in Appendix
B

\displaystyle-\nu\Delta\boldsymbol{\varphi^{*}}+L^{*}_{e}(\boldsymbol{\varphi^{*}})+\psi^{*}\nabla\theta_{e}+\nabla\pi

\displaystyle=\bar{\lambda}_{i}\boldsymbol{\varphi^{*}}

\displaystyle\text{in }\Omega

(4.36a)

\displaystyle-\kappa\Delta\psi^{*}-\boldsymbol{y}_{e}\cdot\nabla\psi^{*}-\gamma\boldsymbol{\varphi^{*}}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{d}

\displaystyle=\bar{\lambda}_{i}\psi^{*}

\displaystyle\text{in }\Omega

(4.36b)

\displaystyle\text{div }\boldsymbol{\varphi^{*}}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\text{in }\Omega

(4.36c)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varphi^{*}}|_{\Gamma}=0,\ \psi^{*}|_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\text{on }\Gamma.

(4.36d)

and in addition, the over-determined conditions in (
4.35
):

\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}\equiv 0\text{ in }\omega,\quad\partial_{\nu}\psi^{*}|_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}\equiv 0.

(4.36e)

Write
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}=\{\varphi^{*(1)},\varphi^{*(2)},\dots,\varphi^{*(d)}\}
for the
d
-components of the vector
\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}
. Since
\boldsymbol{e}_{d}=\{0,\dots,0,1\}
, the property
\varphi^{*d}=0
in
\omega
, contained in (
4.36e
), used in (
4.36b
) implies:

\displaystyle\kappa\Delta\psi^{*}+\boldsymbol{y}_{e}\cdot\nabla\psi^{*}

\displaystyle=-\bar{\lambda}_{i}\psi^{*}

\displaystyle\text{in }\omega

(4.37a)

\displaystyle\psi^{*}|_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}=0,\ \partial_{\nu}\psi^{*}|_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\text{on }\widetilde{\Gamma}.

(4.37b)

recalling the boundary conditions for
\psi^{*}
in (
4.36d
) and (
4.36e
) for the
\psi^{*}
-problem defined on
\omega
, with
\widetilde{\Gamma}\subset\partial\omega
, as in Fig 1. It is then a standard result
[
17
]
,
[
M.1
, Sect 19, pp 59-61]
,
[
36
, p 3]
,
[
51
]
that the over-determined problem (
4.37
) implies

\psi^{*}\equiv 0\text{ in }\omega.

(4.38)

Then (
4.38
) and the full strength of (
4.36d
) give the over-determination

\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*}\equiv 0\text{ in }\omega,\ \psi^{*}\equiv 0\text{ in }\omega

(4.39)

for problem (
4.36
). By means of such over-determination, we can apply the Unique Continuation Property
[
89
, Theorem 5]
recalled as Theorem
B.1
in Appendix
B
and conclude that

\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}=\{\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{*},\psi^{*}\}\equiv 0\text{ in }\Omega,\quad\pi\equiv\text{const.}\text{ in }\Omega,

(4.40)

or recalling (
4.34
)

\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}_{i\ell_{i}}=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell_{i}-1}\alpha_{j}\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}_{ij}\text{ in }\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)=\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\times L^{q}(\Omega);

(4.41)

i.e. the set
\displaystyle\{\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}_{i1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}_{i\ell_{i}}\}
is linearly dependent on
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)
. But this is false by the very selection of such eigenvectors, see above (
4.1
). Thus the two conditions in (
4.33
) cannot hold simultaneously. The claim is proved. Hence it is possible to select, in infinitely many ways, boundary functions
\displaystyle f_{1},\dots,f_{K}\in{\mathcal{F}}\subset L^{q}(\Gamma)
for
q\geq 2
and interior
d
-vectors
\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{u}_{K}
in
\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)
, such that the Kalman algebraic full rank conditions (
4.31
) hold true. Indeed they may be chosen independent of
i
. Start with (
4.31
) for
\bar{\imath}
such that
\displaystyle\ell_{\bar{\imath}}=K=\max\left\{\ell_{i},\ i=1,\dots,M\right\}
yielding
\displaystyle f_{1},\dots,f_{K}
which are linearly independent. They also work in other
i
’s.
∎

Remark 4.1
.

The UCP in
[
89
, Theorem 5]
reported as Theorem
B.1
in Appendix
B
, refers to problem (
4.36a-c
), without the B.C. (
4.36d
), with the a-priori over-determination
\psi^{*}\equiv 0
in
\omega
, and
\displaystyle\{\varphi^{*(1)},\dots,\varphi^{*(d-1)}\}\equiv 0
in
\omega
, thus not involving the last
d
-component
\varphi^{*(d)}\equiv 0
. In contrast, in the setting of proving the above Claim, we need
\varphi^{*(d)}\equiv 0
in
\omega
in order to deduce
\psi^{*}\equiv 0
in
\omega
. The present proof on the validity of the rank conditions (
4.31
), ultimately relying on the UCP of
[
89
, Theorem 5]
in the Appendix
B
requires the full strength of the fluid vectors
\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell_{i}}
, each possessing
d
-components. No geometrical assumptions are involved for the pair
\{\omega,\widetilde{\Gamma}\}
, except for
\omega
being an interior subdomain touching the boundary
\widetilde{\Gamma}
as in Fig 1. In Appendix
C
, we report an improvement requiring only
(d-1)
components from the fluid vectors
\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{u}_{\ell_{i}}
to include necessarily the
d
th
components. ∎

Remark 4.2
.

We have established Theorem
4.1
under the simplifying semisimple assumption (
4.11
). However, Theorem
4.1
holds true in full generality. The corresponding proof is lengthy and technical. It may be given by following the scheme given in
[
56
]
,
[
57
]
,
[
62
]
for the Navier-Stokes equations. It requires use of the controllability criterion for a finite dimensional pair
\{A,B\}
with
A
in Jordan canonical form
[
18
]
,
[
8
]
. Henceforth, we proceed by taking that Theorem
4.1
is true in full generality. ∎

As a consequence Theorem
4.1
, we obtain the following fundamental result on the uniform stabilization with arbitrarily preassigned decay rate, of the original unstable, finite dimensional
\boldsymbol{w}_{N}
-problem (
3.6a
), the counterpart of
[
61
, Theorem 5.1]
.

Theorem 4.2
.

Let
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{M}
be the unstable distinct eigenvalues of the operator
\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{q}\ (=\mathbb{A}_{q,N}^{u})
as in (
1.34
) with geometric multiplicity
\ell_{i},\ i=1,\dots,M
, and set
K=\sup\{\ell_{i};i=1,\dots,M\}
. Let
\widetilde{\Gamma}
be an open connected subset of the boundary
\Gamma
of positive surface measure and
\omega
be a localized collar supported by
\widetilde{\Gamma}
(Fig. 1). Let
q\geq 2
. Given
\gamma_{1}>0
arbitrarily large, we can construct two
K
-dimensional controllers: a boundary control
v=v_{N}
acting with support on
\widetilde{\Gamma}
, of the form given by

v=v_{N}=\sum^{K}_{k=1}\nu_{k}(t)f_{k},\ f_{k}\in{\mathcal{F}}\subset W^{2-{}^{1}\!/_{q},q}(\Gamma),\ q\geq 2,

(4.42)

{\mathcal{F}}
defined in (
4.21
),
q\geq 2
,
f_{k}
supported on
\widetilde{\Gamma}
, and an interior control
\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{u}_{N}
acting on
\omega
, of the form given by

\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{u}_{N}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mu_{k}(t)\boldsymbol{u}_{k},\quad\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\in\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}\subset\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega),\quad\mu_{k}(t)=\text{scalar,}

(4.43)

thus with interior vectors
[\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{u}_{K}]
in the smooth subspace
\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}
of
\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega),\ 2\leq q<\infty
, supported on
\omega
, such that, once inserted in the finite dimensional projected
\boldsymbol{w}_{N}
-system in (
3.6a
), yields the system

\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime}_{N}=\mathbb{A}^{u}_{q,N}\boldsymbol{w}_{N}+P_{N}\begin{bmatrix}P_{q}\left(m\left(\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mu_{k}(t)\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\right)\right)\\
{\mathcal{B}}_{q}D\left(\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\nu_{k}(t)f_{k}\right)\end{bmatrix},

(4.44)

whose solution then satisfies the estimate

\left\lVert\boldsymbol{w}_{N}(t)\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)}+\left\lVert v_{N}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{q}(\widetilde{\Gamma})}+\left\lVert v^{\prime}_{N}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{q}(\widetilde{\Gamma})}+\\
\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}_{N}(t)\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\omega)}+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}_{N}(t)\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\omega)}\leq C_{\gamma_{1}}e^{-\gamma_{1}t}\left\lVert P_{N}w_{0}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)},\quad t\geq 0.

(4.45)

Moreover, such controllers
v=v_{N}
and
\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{u}_{N}
may be chosen in feedback form: that is, with reference to the explicit expressions (
4.42
) for
v
and (
4.43
) for
\boldsymbol{u}
, of the form
\nu_{k}(t)=\left\langle\boldsymbol{w}_{N}(t),\boldsymbol{p}_{k}\right\rangle_{{}_{\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}}}
and
\mu_{k}(t)=\left\langle\boldsymbol{w}_{N}(t),\boldsymbol{q}_{k}\right\rangle_{{}_{\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}}}
for suitable vectors
\boldsymbol{p}_{k}\in\left(\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}\right)^{*}\subset\boldsymbol{L}^{q^{\prime}}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\times L^{q^{\prime}}(\Omega)
,
\boldsymbol{q}_{k}\in\left(\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}\right)^{*}\subset\boldsymbol{L}^{q^{\prime}}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\times L^{q^{\prime}}(\Omega)
depending on
\gamma_{1}
, where
\left\langle\ ,\ \right\rangle
denotes the duality pairing
\displaystyle\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}\times\left(\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}\right)^{*}
.

In conclusion,
\boldsymbol{w}_{N}
in (
4.45
) is the solution of the equation (
4.44
) on
\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}
rewritten explicitly as

\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime}_{N}=\mathbb{A}^{u}_{q,N}\boldsymbol{w}_{N}+P_{N}\begin{bmatrix}P_{q}\left(m\left(\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left\langle\boldsymbol{w}_{N}(t),\boldsymbol{q}_{k}\right\rangle_{\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}}\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\right)\right)\\
{\mathcal{B}}_{q}D\left(\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left\langle\boldsymbol{w}_{N}(t),\boldsymbol{p}_{k}\right\rangle_{\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}}f_{k}\right)\end{bmatrix},

(4.46)

f_{k}
supported on
\widetilde{\Gamma}
,
\boldsymbol{u}_{k}
supported on
\omega
, rewritten in turn as

\boldsymbol{w}^{\prime}_{N}=\overline{A}^{u}\boldsymbol{w}_{N},\ \boldsymbol{w}_{N}(t)=e^{\overline{A}^{u}t}P_{N}\boldsymbol{w}_{0},\ \boldsymbol{w}_{N}(0)=P_{N}\boldsymbol{w}_{0}\quad\text{on }\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}.

(4.47)

Proof.

The technical proof is similar circumstances was given in
[
56
]
,
[
57
]
,
[
62
]
. Thus, we give here only some insight. The key new fact is the controllability test of Theorem
4.1
. Having established the controllability condition for the pair
\{\Lambda,B\}
, then by the well-known Popov’s criterion on finite dimensional theory (see e.g.
[
94
, Theorem 2.9 p 44]
), there exists a feedback matrix
Q:2K\times N
, such that the spectrum of the matrix
[\Lambda+BQ]
may be arbitrarily preassigned, in particular, to lie arbitrarily on the left half-plane
\displaystyle\{\lambda:Re\ \lambda<-\gamma_{1}<-Re\ \lambda_{N+1}\}
, with
\gamma_{1}>0
preassigned, as desired. The resulting closed-loop system is

(\hat{\boldsymbol{w}}_{N})^{\prime}=\Lambda\hat{\boldsymbol{w}}_{N}+BQ\hat{\boldsymbol{w}}_{N},\ \begin{bmatrix}\hat{\nu}_{K}\\
\hat{\mu}_{K}\end{bmatrix}=Q\hat{\boldsymbol{w}}_{N}=\text{ feedback control}

(4.48)

Q=\begin{bmatrix}\text{ row }\hat{p}_{1}\\
\vdots\\
\text{ row }\hat{p}_{K}\\
\text{ row }\hat{q}_{1}\\
\vdots\\
\text{ row }\hat{q}_{K}\end{bmatrix}:2K\times N;\ \hat{\nu}_{K}=\begin{bmatrix}\nu^{1}_{K}=\left\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1},\hat{\boldsymbol{w}}_{N}\right\rangle\\
\vdots\\
\nu^{k}_{K}=\left\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{p}}_{k},\hat{\boldsymbol{w}}_{N}\right\rangle\\
\vdots\\
\nu^{K}_{K}=\left\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{p}}_{K},\hat{\boldsymbol{w}}_{N}\right\rangle\end{bmatrix},\ \hat{\mu}_{K}=\begin{bmatrix}\mu^{1}_{K}=\left\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{q}}_{1},\hat{\boldsymbol{w}}_{N}\right\rangle\\
\vdots\\
\mu^{k}_{K}=\left\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{q}}_{k},\hat{\boldsymbol{w}}_{N}\right\rangle\\
\vdots\\
\mu^{K}_{K}=\left\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{q}}_{K},\hat{\boldsymbol{w}}_{N}\right\rangle\end{bmatrix}

(4.49)

in the
\mathbb{C}^{N}
-inner product. Thus, returning from
\mathbb{C}^{N}\times\mathbb{C}^{N}
back to
\displaystyle\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}\times\left[\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}\right]^{*}
, there exist vectors
\boldsymbol{p}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{p}_{K}
and
\boldsymbol{q}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{q}_{K}
in
\displaystyle\left[\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}\right]^{*}
such that

\nu_{K}^{k}=\left\langle\boldsymbol{w}_{N},\boldsymbol{p}_{k}\right\rangle,\ \mu_{K}^{k}=\left\langle\boldsymbol{w}_{N},\boldsymbol{q}_{k}\right\rangle,\ k=1,\dots,K

(4.50)

where
\left\langle\ ,\ \right\rangle
denotes the duality paring
\displaystyle\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}\times\left[\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}\right)^{u}_{N}\right]^{*}
. This way, the closed loop system (
4.46
) corresponds to the
\mathbb{C}^{N}
-system (
4.48
). (
4.49
).

(5.1)

\displaystyle J\boldsymbol{w}

6
The linearized feedback operator
\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}
is the generator of a s.c. analytic, uniformly stable semigroup
\displaystyle e^{\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}t}
on
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)
and
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)
.

Theorem 6.1
.

The operator
\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}
in (
5.3b
) generates a s.c., analytic semigroup
\displaystyle e^{\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}t}
on
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\equiv\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\times L^{q}(\Omega)
as well as in
\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)\equiv\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega)\times B^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega)
.

Proof.

First on
\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)
:
As the perturbation operator
\Pi
in (
5.5a
) involves bounded operators
\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{\gamma},\ {\mathcal{C}}_{\theta_{e}},\ J
(see (
1.21
), (
1.22
), (
5.1
)), as well as the benign operator
\displaystyle A_{o,q}
, in (
1.15
) with
\displaystyle A_{o,q}A^{-{}^{1}\!/_{2}}_{q}
bounded on
\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)
, it suffices to show that the streamlined operator
\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{A}}_{{}_{F,q}}
in (
5.5a
) generates a s.c. analytic semigroup
\displaystyle e^{\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}t}
on
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)
. To this end, it will be more convenient to show equivalently that the adjoint
\hat{\mathbb{A}}_{{}_{F,q}}^{*}
is the generator of a s.c. analytic semigroup
\displaystyle e^{\hat{\mathbb{A}}^{*}_{{}_{F,q}}t}
on
\displaystyle\left[\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\right]^{*}=\boldsymbol{W}_{\sigma}^{q^{\prime}}(\Omega)=\boldsymbol{L}^{q^{\prime}}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\times L^{q^{\prime}}(\Omega)
, since
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega),\ 1<q<\infty
, is a reflexive space. The adjoint
\hat{\mathbb{A}}_{{}_{F,q}}^{*}
of
\hat{\mathbb{A}}_{{}_{F,q}}
in (
5.5a
) is given by

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{A}}_{{}_{F,q}}^{*}\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\\
v_{2}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}-A^{*}_{q}&0\\
0&-{\mathcal{B}}^{*}_{q}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\\
v_{2}\end{bmatrix}+F^{*}D^{*}{\mathcal{B}}^{*}_{q}v_{2}

(6.1a)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}\left(\hat{\mathbb{A}}^{*}_{{}_{F,q}}\right)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{D}}(A^{*}_{q})\times{\mathcal{D}}(B^{*}_{q}),\ {\mathcal{D}}(B^{*}_{q})={\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{B}}^{*}_{q})

(6.1b)

by (
1.23c
). Since the operator

\hat{\mathbb{A}}_{q}^{*}=\begin{bmatrix}-A^{*}_{q}&0\\
0&-{\mathcal{B}}^{*}_{q}\end{bmatrix},\ \boldsymbol{W}_{\sigma}^{q^{\prime}}(\Omega)\supset{\mathcal{D}}\left(\hat{\mathbb{A}}^{*}_{q}\right)={\mathcal{D}}(A_{q}^{*})\times{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{B}}_{q}^{*})\longrightarrow\boldsymbol{W}_{\sigma}^{q^{\prime}}(\Omega)

(6.2)

is plainly the generator of a s.c. analytic semigroup
\displaystyle e^{\hat{\mathbb{A}}^{*}_{{}_{F,q}}t}
on
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{W}^{q^{\prime}}_{\sigma}(\Omega)
(Appendix
A
), it will suffice to show that the perturbation operator

F^{*}D^{*}{\mathcal{B}}_{q}^{*}\text{ is }\left(\hat{\mathbb{A}}_{q}^{*}\right)^{\theta_{0}}=\begin{bmatrix}-A^{*\theta_{0}}_{q}&0\\
0&-{\mathcal{B}}^{*\theta_{0}}_{q}\end{bmatrix}-\text{bounded, for some constant }0<\theta_{0}<1

(6.3)

[
68
]
. In our case, it will be
\displaystyle\theta_{0}=1-{}^{1}\!/_{2q}+\varepsilon<1
. In fact, recall via (
1.23b
) that
\displaystyle D^{*}{\mathcal{B}}^{*\gamma}_{q}\in{\mathcal{L}}(L^{q^{\prime}}(\Omega),L^{q^{\prime}}(\Gamma)),\ \gamma={}^{1}\!/_{2q}-\epsilon
. (In (
6.3
) and below we are taking that the fractional powers of
{\mathcal{B}}^{*}_{q}
are well-defined, for otherwise a translation will do it. Alternatively, in (
6.1a
), write
\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}^{*}_{q}=B^{*}_{q}+B^{*}_{o,q}
from (
1.18
) as in (
B.10
) for
\kappa=1
where
\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}\left(B^{*}_{o,q}\right)={\mathcal{D}}\left(B^{*{}^{1}\!/_{2}}_{q}\right)
. Do the argument with
B^{*}_{q}
whose fractional powers are well-defined. This introduces an additional perturbation which is benign, as it can be handled by using

\left\lVert B^{*}_{o,q}v_{2}\right\rVert=\left\lVert\left(B^{*}_{o,q}B^{*-{}^{1}\!/_{2}}_{q}\right)\left(B^{*-{}^{1}\!/_{2}}_{q}B^{*}_{q}\right)v_{2}\right\rVert\leq C\left\lVert B^{*}_{q}v_{2}\right\rVert

as desired.) Next, for
\displaystyle v_{2}\in{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{B}}^{*1-\gamma}_{q})
and
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\in{\mathcal{D}}(A^{*1-\gamma}_{q})
, we estimate since
\displaystyle F^{*}\in{\mathcal{L}}\left(L^{q^{\prime}}(\Gamma),\boldsymbol{W}^{q^{\prime}}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\right)
in the norm of
\boldsymbol{W}_{\sigma}^{q^{\prime}}(\Omega)
:

\displaystyle\left\lVert F^{*}D^{*}{\mathcal{B}}^{*}_{q}v_{2}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\left(F^{*}D^{*}{\mathcal{B}}^{*^{\gamma}}_{q}\right){\mathcal{B}}^{*^{1-\gamma}}_{q}v_{2}\right\rVert\leq C\left\lVert{\mathcal{B}}^{*^{1-\gamma}}_{q}v_{2}\right\rVert

(6.4)

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\left\lVert{\mathcal{B}}^{*^{1-\gamma}}_{q}v_{2}\right\rVert+\left\lVert A^{*^{1-\gamma}}_{q}\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\right\rVert\right]

(6.5)

\displaystyle\leq C\left\lVert\begin{bmatrix}A^{*}_{q}&0\\
0&B^{*}_{q}\end{bmatrix}^{1-\gamma}\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{v}_{1}\\
v_{2}\end{bmatrix}\right\rVert

(6.6)

and (
6.6
) proves (
6.3
). In conclusion: the operator
\displaystyle\mathbb{A}^{*}_{{}_{F,q}}
generates a s.c. analytic semigroup on
\displaystyle\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)^{*}\right)=\boldsymbol{L}^{q^{\prime}}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\times L^{q^{\prime}}(\Omega)
; and hence as this space is reflexive, the operator
\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}
generates a s.c. analytic semigroup on
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)=\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\times L^{q}(\Omega)
.

Next on
\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)\equiv\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega)\times B^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega)
:
(See Remark
1.3
)
From the above conclusion on
\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}^{*}
on
\left(\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\right)^{*}
, it then follows that
\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}^{*}
generates a s.c. analytic semigroup on
\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}(\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}^{*})\equiv{\mathcal{D}}(A^{*}_{q})\times{\mathcal{D}}(B^{*}_{q})
, see (
6.1b
). Then
\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}^{*}
generates a s.c. analytic semigroup on the real interpolation spaces between
\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}(A^{*}_{q})\times{\mathcal{D}}(B^{*}_{q})
and
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{L}^{q^{\prime}}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\times L^{q^{\prime}}(\Omega)
, thus on the space
\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q^{\prime},p}(\Omega)\times B^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q^{\prime},p}(\Omega),\ 1<p<{}^{2q^{\prime}}\!/_{2q^{\prime}-1}
. Recall the version of (
1.11
) for adjoints and get

\left(\boldsymbol{L}^{q^{\prime}}_{\sigma}(\Omega),{\mathcal{D}}(A^{*}_{q})\right)_{1-\frac{1}{p},p}\equiv\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q^{\prime},p}(\Omega),\ 1<p<\frac{2q^{\prime}}{2q^{\prime}-1},\\
1<q^{\prime}<2,\ q>2,\ \frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}=1.

For the second component recall Remark
1.3
. It then finally follows, again by reflexivity of the space, that
\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}
generates a s.c. analytic semigroup on the space
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)\equiv\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega)\times B^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega)
.
∎

The next result proves the sought-after uniform stabilization of the localized feedback
\boldsymbol{w}
-system (
5.3
) or (
5.4
); that is, that the s.c. analytic semigroup
\displaystyle e^{\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}t}
on
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega),\ t\geq 0
or on
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)
, as guaranteed by Theorem
6.1
, is uniformly stable.

Theorem 6.2
.

Under the same setting of Theorem
4.2
concerning in particular the choice of the vectors
\boldsymbol{q}_{k},\boldsymbol{p}_{k},\boldsymbol{u}_{k},f_{k},
the
\boldsymbol{w}
-problem (

), (

) in feedback from is uniformly stable, with a decay rate
\gamma>0,\ Re\ \lambda_{N+1}<-\gamma<0
either in
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\equiv\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\times L^{q}(\Omega)
or else in
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)\equiv\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega)\times B^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega)
.

\left\lVert\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{w}_{f}\\
w_{h}\end{bmatrix}\right\rVert_{(\cdot)}\leq C_{\gamma_{0}}e^{-\gamma_{0}t}\left\lVert\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{w}_{f}(0)\\
w_{h}(0)\end{bmatrix}\right\rVert_{(\cdot)},\ t\geq 0.

(6.7a)

In short, recalling the operator
\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}
in (
5.3b
)

\left\lVert e^{\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}t}\right\rVert_{{\mathcal{L}}(\cdot)}\leq C_{\gamma_{0}}e^{-\gamma_{0}t},\ t\geq 0.

(6.7b)

Here,
(\cdot)
denotes either the space
\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)
or the space
\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)
, see Remark
1.3
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of
[

, making critical use of Theorem

on the uniform stabilization (

-system (

. Next, one examines the impact of the constructive feedback controls in (

) and (

) on the

-dynamics (

), whose explicit solution is given by the variation of parameter formula

\zeta_{N}(t)=e^{\mathbb{A}_{q,N}^{s}}\zeta_{N}(0)+\int_{0}^{t}e^{\mathbb{A}_{q,N}^{s}(t-\tau)}(I-P_{N})\begin{bmatrix}P_{q}(m\boldsymbol{u}_{N})(\tau)\\[2.84526pt]
{\mathcal{B}}_{q}Dv_{N}(\tau)\end{bmatrix}d\tau.

(6.8)

where

\left\lVert e^{\mathbb{A}_{q,N}^{s}}\right\rVert_{{\mathcal{L}}(\cdot)}\leq C_{\gamma_{0}}e^{-\gamma_{0}t},\ 0\geq t,\ 0<\gamma_{0}<\left\lvert Re\ \lambda_{N+1}\right\rvert;

(6.9)

while

decay with an arbitrary large exponential rate

as in (

). The validity of (

) on

follows by (

), restricted to the stable subspace

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}(\mathbb{A}_{q})={\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{A}}_{q})\times{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{B}}_{q})={\mathcal{D}}(A_{q})\times{\mathcal{D}}(B_{q})
, see (
1.34
).

Details are in
[

, Section 10]
.

7
Maximal

Consider the following abstract dynamics

\zeta_{t}=\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}\zeta+\chi,\quad\zeta(0)=\zeta_{0},\quad\text{in }\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)

(7.1)

\zeta(t)=e^{\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}}\zeta_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}(t-s)}\chi(s)ds

(7.2)

The main result of the present section is

Theorem 7.1
.

With reference to the
\boldsymbol{w}
-problem (

) in feedback form defining the feedback operator
\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}
, assume the setting of Theorem

up to

;

\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}\in MReg\left(L^{p}\left(0,\infty;\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\right)\right).

(7.3)

That is,

(L\chi)(t)=\int_{0}^{t}e^{\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}(t-s)}\chi(s)ds

(7.4)

continuous:

\displaystyle\hskip 17.07182pt\in L^{p}\left(0,\infty;{\mathcal{D}}\left(\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}\right)\right)\cap W^{1,p}(0,\infty;\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\equiv L^{p}\left(0,\infty;\boldsymbol{W}^{2,q}(\Omega)\right)\times L^{p}\left(0,\infty;W^{2,q}(\Omega)\right)

Proof.

Again, as in the proof of Theorem
6.1
about analyticity of
\displaystyle e^{\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}t}

), (

\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{A}}^{*}_{{}_{F,q}}

. To establish this, we return to estimate (

) showing statement (

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{q}^{*}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{A}}_{{}_{F,q}}^{*}\in MReg(L^{p}(0,T;\boldsymbol{W}^{q^{\prime}}_{\sigma}(\Omega))),\ T<\infty

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{A}}_{{}_{F,q}}\in MReg(L^{p}(0,T;\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega))),\ T<\infty
. Then we obtain
\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}\in MReg(L^{p}(0,T;\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega))),\ T<\infty
. But
\displaystyle e^{\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}t}
is uniformly stable on
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)
by Theorem
6.2
. Hence we obtain
\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}\in MReg(L^{p}(0,\infty;\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)))
, and Theorem
7.1
is proved.
∎

We next examine the regularity of the term
\displaystyle e^{\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}t}\zeta_{0}
due to the initial condition
\zeta_{0}
as in (
7.2
).

Theorem 7.2
.

(i)

Let
\displaystyle 1<p<\frac{2q^{\prime}}{2q^{\prime}-1},\ 1<q^{\prime}\leq 2,\ 2\leq q,\ \frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}=1

e^{\mathbb{A}^{*}_{{}_{F,q}}t}:\text{ continuous }\equiv\boldsymbol{V}^{q^{\prime},p}_{b}(\Omega)\equiv\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q^{\prime},p}(\Omega)\times B^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q^{\prime},p}(\Omega)\\
=(\boldsymbol{L}^{q^{\prime}}_{\sigma}(\Omega),{\mathcal{D}}(A^{*}_{q}))_{1-\frac{1}{p},p}\times(L^{q^{\prime}}(\Omega),{\mathcal{D}}(B^{*}_{q}))_{1-\frac{1}{p},p}\\
\longrightarrow\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q^{\prime},\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q^{\prime}}\equiv L^{p}(0,\infty;{\mathcal{D}}(\mathbb{A}^{*}_{{}_{F,q}}))\cap W^{1,p}(0,\infty;\boldsymbol{W}^{q^{\prime}}_{\sigma}(\Omega))

(7.7)

(ii)

Consider the original s.c. analytic feedback semigroup
\displaystyle e^{\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}t}
on
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)

6.7b
). Let
\displaystyle 1<p<\frac{2q}{2q-1},\ 2\leq q

e^{\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}t}:\text{ continuous }\equiv\boldsymbol{V}^{q,p}_{b}(\Omega)\equiv\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega)\times B^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega)\\

\longrightarrow\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}\equiv L^{p}\left(0,\infty;{\mathcal{D}}\left(\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}\right)\right)\cap W^{1,p}\left(0,\infty;\boldsymbol{W}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\right)

Proof.

.

(8.6)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{f}

Proof of Theorem
8.2

Step 1:
We start from the definition (

) of
\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}(\boldsymbol{b}_{0},\boldsymbol{f})
and invoke the maximal regularity properties (

) of Theorem

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\mathcal{F}}(\boldsymbol{b}_{0},\boldsymbol{f})\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert e^{\mathbb{A}_{F,q}t}\boldsymbol{b}_{0}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}}

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\int_{0}^{t}e^{\mathbb{A}_{F,q}(t-\tau)}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathcal{N}}_{q}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}(\tau)\\[2.84526pt]
{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{f}_{1}]f_{2}(\tau)\end{bmatrix}d\tau\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}}

(8.13)

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg[\left\lVert\boldsymbol{b}_{0}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)}+

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\mathcal{N}}_{q}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{p}\big(0,\infty;\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\big)}+\left\lVert{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{f}_{1}]f_{2}\right\rVert_{L^{p}\big(0,\infty;L^{q}(\Omega)\big)}\bigg].

(8.14)

Step 2:
Regarding the term
\displaystyle{\mathcal{N}}_{q}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}
we can invoke
[
60
, Eq (8.19)]
to obtain

\left\lVert{\mathcal{N}}_{q}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{p}\big(0,\infty;\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\big)}\leq C\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\right\rVert^{2}_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}},\ \boldsymbol{f}_{1}\in\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}.

(8.15)

Regarding the term
\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{f}_{1}]f_{2}
, we can trace the proof in
[
60
, from (8.10)
\longrightarrow
(8.18)]
(which yielded estimate (
8.14
)). For the sake of clarity, we shall reproduce the computations in the present case with
\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{f}_{1}]f_{2}=\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\cdot\nabla f_{2}

1.20
),
mutatis mutandis
. We shall obtain

\left\lVert{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{f}_{1}]f_{2}\right\rVert_{L^{p}\big(0,\infty;L^{q}(\Omega)\big)}\leq C\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}\left\lVert f_{2}\right\rVert_{X^{\infty}_{p,q}},\ \boldsymbol{f}_{1}\in\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma},f_{2}\in X^{\infty}_{p,q}.

In fact, let us compute from (
1.20
)

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{f}_{1}]f_{2}\right\rVert_{L^{p}\big(0,\infty;L^{q}(\Omega)\big)}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\cdot\nabla f_{2}\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{q}(\Omega)}dt

(8.17)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg\{\int_{\Omega}\left\lvert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}(t,x)\right\rvert^{q}\left\lvert\nabla f_{2}(t,x)\right\rvert^{q}d\Omega\bigg\}^{{}^{p}\!/_{q}}dt

(8.18)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg\{\bigg[\sup_{\Omega}

\displaystyle\left\lvert\nabla f_{2}(t,x)\right\rvert^{q}\bigg]^{{}^{1}\!/_{q}}\bigg[\int_{\Omega}\left\lvert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}(t,x)\right\rvert^{q}d\Omega\bigg]^{{}^{1}\!/_{q}}\bigg\}^{p}dt

(8.19)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\lVert\nabla f_{2}(t,\cdot)\right\rVert^{p}_{\boldsymbol{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}(t,\cdot)\right\rVert^{p}_{\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)}dt

(8.20)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{0\leq t\leq\infty}

\displaystyle\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}(t,\cdot)\right\rVert^{p}_{\boldsymbol{L}_{\sigma}^{q}(\Omega)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\lVert\nabla f_{2}(t,\cdot)\right\rVert^{p}_{\boldsymbol{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}dt

(8.21)

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega))}\left\lVert\nabla f_{2}\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(0,\infty;\boldsymbol{L}^{\infty}(\Omega))}.

(8.22)

See also
[
60
, Eq (8.14)]
. The following embeddings hold true (see the stronger Eq (
2.16
)):

(i)

[
32
, Proposition 4.3, p 1406 with
\mu=0,s=\infty,r=q
]
so that the required formula reduces to
1\geq{}^{1}\!/_{p}
, as desired

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\in\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\hookrightarrow\boldsymbol{f}_{1}

\displaystyle\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega))

(8.23a)

\displaystyle\text{ so that, }\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}};

(8.23b)

(ii)

[
41
, Theorem 2.4.4, p 74 requiring

-boundary]

W^{1,q}(\Omega)\subset L^{\infty}(\Omega)\text{ for q}>\text{dim }\Omega=d,\ d=2,3,

(8.24)

so that, with

:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla f_{2}\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(0,\infty;\boldsymbol{L}^{\infty}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\lVert\nabla f_{2}\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(0,\infty;\boldsymbol{W}^{1,q}(\Omega))}\leq C\left\lVert f_{2}\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(0,\infty;W^{2,q}(\Omega))}

(8.25)

\displaystyle\leq C\left\lVert f_{2}\right\rVert^{p}_{X^{\infty}_{p,q}}.

(8.26)

In going from (
8.25
) to (
8.26
) we have recalled the definition of

in (

) and (

) into the RHS of (

).

Step 3:
Substituting estimates (
8.15
) and (
8.16
) on the RHS of (
8.14
), we finally obtain

\left\lVert{\mathcal{F}}(\boldsymbol{b}_{0},\boldsymbol{f}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}}\leq C\Big\{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{b}_{0}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)}+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}\big(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}+\left\lVert f_{2}\right\rVert_{X^{\infty}_{p,q}}\big)\Big\}.

(8.27)

See
[
60
, Eqt (8.20)]
.

Step 4:
We now impose restrictions on the data on the RHS of (
8.27
):
\boldsymbol{b}_{0}
is in a ball of radius
r_{1}>0
in
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega)\times B^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega)
and
\boldsymbol{f}=\{\boldsymbol{f}_{1},f_{2}\}
lies in a ball of radius
r>0
in
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}
. We further demand that the final result
\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}(\boldsymbol{b}_{0},\boldsymbol{f})
shall lie in a ball of radius
r>0
in
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}
. Thus, we obtain from (
8.27
)

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\mathcal{F}}(\boldsymbol{b}_{0},\boldsymbol{f})\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big\{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{b}_{0}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)}+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}\big(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}+\left\lVert f_{2}\right\rVert_{X^{\infty}_{p,q}}\big)\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq C(r_{1}+r\cdot r)\leq r.

(8.28)

This implies

Cr^{2}-r+Cr_{1}\leq 0\quad\text{or}\quad\frac{1-\sqrt{1-4C^{2}r_{1}}}{2C}\leq r\leq\frac{1+\sqrt{1-4C^{2}r_{1}}}{2C}

(8.29)

whereby

\begin{Bmatrix}\text{ range of values of r }\end{Bmatrix}\longrightarrow\text{ interval }\Big[0,\frac{1}{C}\Big],\text{ as }r_{1}\searrow 0,

(8.30)

a constraint which is guaranteed by taking

r_{1}\leq\frac{1}{4C^{2}},\ C\text{ being the constant in }(\ref{8.28})\ \left(\text{w.l.o.g. }C>\frac{1}{4}\right).

(8.31)

We have thus established that by taking
r_{1}
as in (
8.31
) and subsequently
r
as in (
8.29
), then the map

{\mathcal{F}}(\boldsymbol{b}_{0},\boldsymbol{f})\text{ takes: }\begin{Bmatrix}\text{ ball in }\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)\\
\text{of radius }r_{1}\end{Bmatrix}\times\begin{Bmatrix}\text{ ball in }\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}\\
\text{of radius }r\end{Bmatrix}\text{ into }\\
\begin{Bmatrix}\text{ ball in }\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}\\
\text{of radius }r\end{Bmatrix},\ d<q,\ 1<p<\frac{2q}{2q-1}.

(8.32)

This establishes Theorem
8.2
. ∎

Proof of Theorem
8.3

Step 1:
For
\boldsymbol{f}=\{\boldsymbol{f}_{1},f_{2}\},\boldsymbol{g}=\{\boldsymbol{g}_{1},g_{2}\}
both in the ball of
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}
of radius
r
obtained in the proof of Theorem
8.2
, we estimate from (
8.10
):

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\mathcal{F}}(\boldsymbol{b}_{0},\boldsymbol{f})-{\mathcal{F}}(\boldsymbol{b}_{0},\boldsymbol{g})\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}}=

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\left\lVert\int_{0}^{t}e^{\mathbb{A}_{F,q}(t-\tau)}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathcal{N}}_{q}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}(\tau)-{\mathcal{N}}_{q}\boldsymbol{g}_{1}(\tau)\\[2.84526pt]
{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{f}_{1}]f_{2}(\tau)-{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{g}_{1}]g_{2}(\tau)\end{bmatrix}d\tau\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{m}\Big[\left\lVert{\mathcal{N}}_{q}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}-{\mathcal{N}}_{q}\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{p}\big(0,\infty;\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\big)}+\left\lVert{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{f}_{1}]f_{2}-{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{g}_{1}]g_{2}\right\rVert_{L^{p}(0,\infty;L^{q}(\Omega))}\Big]

(8.33)

after invoking the maximal regularity property (
7.6
) of Theorem
7.1
, as in (
8.14
).

Step 2:
As to the first term of the RHS of (
8.33
), we can invoke
[
60
, Eq (8.41)]
and obtain

\left\lVert{\mathcal{N}}_{q}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}-{\mathcal{N}}_{q}\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{p}\big(0,\infty;\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\big)}\leq 2^{{}^{1}\!/_{p}}C^{{}^{1}\!/_{p}}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}\left(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}\right.\\
\left.+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}\right).

(8.34)

Regarding the second term on the RHS of (
8.33
) involving
{\mathcal{M}}_{q}
, we can track the proof of
[
60
, from (8.28) to (8.41)]
(which yielded estimate (
8.34
))
mutatis mutandis
. For the sake of clarity, we shall reproduce the computations in the present case, recalling from (
1.22
) that
\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{f}_{1}]f_{2}=\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\cdot\nabla f_{2},{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{g}_{1}]g_{2}=\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\cdot\nabla g_{2}
. We shall obtain

\left\lVert{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{f}_{1}]f_{2}-{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{g}_{1}]g_{2}\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}\big(0,\infty;L^{q}(\Omega)\big)}\leq C\Big\{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert^{p}_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}\left\lVert f_{2}\right\rVert^{p}_{X^{\infty}_{p,q}}\\
+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert^{p}_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}\left\lVert f_{2}-g_{2}\right\rVert^{p}_{X^{\infty}_{p,q}}\Big\}.

(8.35)

In fact, adding and subtracting

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{f}_{1}]f_{2}-{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{g}_{1}]g_{2}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\cdot\nabla f_{2}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\cdot\nabla g_{2}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\cdot\nabla f_{2}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\cdot\nabla f_{2}+\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\cdot\nabla f_{2}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\cdot\nabla g_{2}

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{f}_{1}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1})\cdot\nabla f_{2}+\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\cdot\nabla(f_{2}-g_{2})=A+B.

(8.36)

Thus, using
(*):\ \left\lvert A+B\right\rvert^{p}\leq 2^{p}\big[\left\lvert A\right\rvert^{p}+\left\lvert B\right\rvert^{p}\big]

[
82
, p. 12]
, we estimate

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{f}_{1}]f_{2}-{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{g}_{1}]g_{2}\right\rVert_{L^{p}\big(0,\infty;L^{q}(\Omega)\big)}^{p}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg\{\bigg[\int_{\Omega}\left\lvert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\cdot\nabla f_{2}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\cdot\nabla g_{2}\right\rvert^{q}d\Omega\bigg]^{{}^{1}\!/_{q}}\bigg\}^{p}dt

(8.37)

(by (
8.36
))

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg[\int_{\Omega}\left\lvert A+B\right\rvert^{q}d\Omega\bigg]^{{}^{p}\!/_{q}}dt

(8.38)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p}\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg\{\int_{\Omega}\big[\left\lvert A\right\rvert^{q}+\left\lvert B\right\rvert^{q}\big]d\Omega\bigg\}^{{}^{p}\!/_{q}}dt

(8.39)

\displaystyle=2^{p}\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg\{\Big[\int_{\Omega}\left\lvert A\right\rvert^{q}d\Omega+\int_{\Omega}\left\lvert B\right\rvert^{q}d\Omega\Big]^{{}^{1}\!/_{q}}\bigg\}^{p}dt

(8.40)

\displaystyle=2^{p}\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg\{\Big[\left\lVert A\right\rVert^{q}_{L^{q}(\Omega)}+\left\lVert B\right\rVert^{q}_{L^{q}(\Omega)}\Big]^{{}^{1}\!/_{q}}\bigg\}^{p}dt

(8.41)

\displaystyle\left(\text{by }(*)\mbox{ with }p\to\frac{1}{q}\right)\qquad

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p}\cdot 2^{{}^{p}\!/_{q}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big\{\left\lVert A\right\rVert_{L^{q}(\Omega)}+\left\lVert B\right\rVert_{L^{q}(\Omega)}\Big\}^{p}dt

(8.42)

\displaystyle(\text{by }(*))\qquad

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p+p+{}^{p}\!/_{q}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big[\left\lVert A\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{q}(\Omega)}+\left\lVert B\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{q}(\Omega)}\Big]dt

(8.43)

\displaystyle=2^{p+p+{}^{p}\!/_{q}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big[\left\lVert(\boldsymbol{f}_{1}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1})\cdot\nabla f_{2}\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{q}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\cdot\nabla(f_{2}-g_{2})\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{q}(\Omega)}\Big]dt

(8.44)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p+p+{}^{p}\!/_{q}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big\{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert^{p}_{\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)}\left\lVert\nabla f_{2}\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{q}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert^{p}_{\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)}\left\lVert\nabla(f_{2}-g_{2})\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{q}(\Omega)}\Big\}dt,

(8.45)

recalling the definitions of
A
and
B
in (
8.36
) in passing from (
8.43
) to (
8.44
).
[
60
, This is the counterpart Eq (8.36)]
. Next we proceed by majorizing the
L^{q}(\Omega)
-norm for
\nabla f_{2}
and
\nabla(f_{2}-g_{2})
by the
L^{\infty}(\Omega)
-norm. We obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{f}_{1}]f_{2}-{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{g}_{1}]g_{2}\right\rVert_{L^{p}\big(0,\infty;L^{q}(\Omega)\big)}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\text{RHS of (\ref{8.45})}

\displaystyle\hskip-99.58464pt\leq c2^{p+p+{}^{p}\!/_{q}}\bigg\{\sup_{0\leq t\leq\infty}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert^{p}_{\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\lVert\nabla f_{2}\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt+\sup_{0\leq t\leq\infty}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert^{p}_{\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\lVert\nabla(f_{2}-g_{2})\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}dt\bigg\}

(8.46)

\displaystyle\hskip-99.58464pt\leq c2^{p+p+{}^{p}\!/_{q}}\Big\{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega))}\left\lVert\nabla f_{2}\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(0,\infty;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\hskip-52.63777pt+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega))}\left\lVert\nabla(f_{2}-g_{2})\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(0,\infty;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}\Big\}

(8.47)

by (
8.23b
) and (

)

\displaystyle\hskip-99.58464pt\leq C\Big\{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}^{p}\left\lVert f_{2}\right\rVert_{X^{\infty}_{p,q}}^{p}+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}^{p}\left\lVert f_{2}-g_{2}\right\rVert_{X^{\infty}_{p,q}}^{p}\Big\},

(8.48)

counterpart of
[

, Eq (8.39)]
.

We substitute estimate (

) and estimate (

) on the RHS of (

) and obtain via
(*)

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\mathcal{F}}(\boldsymbol{b}_{0},\boldsymbol{f})-{\mathcal{F}}(\boldsymbol{b}_{0},\boldsymbol{g})\right\rVert^{p}_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p}\widetilde{m}^{p}\Big\{\left\lVert{\mathcal{N}}_{q}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}-{\mathcal{N}}_{q}\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}\big(0,\infty;L^{q}(\Omega)\big)}

\displaystyle+\left\lVert{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{f}_{1}]f_{2}-{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{g}_{1}]g_{2}\right\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(0,\infty;L^{q}(\Omega))}\Big\}

\displaystyle\hskip-71.13188pt\leq C_{p}\widetilde{m}^{p}\Big\{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert^{p}_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}\Big(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}\Big)^{p}

\displaystyle\hskip-62.59596pt+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert^{p}_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}\left\lVert f_{2}\right\rVert^{p}_{X^{\infty}_{p,q}}+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert^{p}_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}\left\lVert f_{2}-g_{2}\right\rVert^{p}_{X^{\infty}_{p,q}}\Big\}.

This is the counterpart of
[
60
, Eq (8.42)]
.

Step 4:
Next pick the points
\boldsymbol{f}=\{\boldsymbol{f}_{1},f_{2}\}
and
\boldsymbol{g}=\{\boldsymbol{g}_{1},g_{2}\}
in a ball of
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}

:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}+\left\lVert f_{2}\right\rVert_{X^{\infty}_{p,q}}<R

\displaystyle\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}+\left\lVert g_{2}\right\rVert_{X^{\infty}_{p,q}}<R.

Then (
8.51
), (
8.52
) used (
8.50
) implies

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\mathcal{F}}(\boldsymbol{b}_{0},\boldsymbol{f})-{\mathcal{F}}(\boldsymbol{b}_{0},\boldsymbol{g})\right\rVert^{p}_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\Big\{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert^{p}_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}\big[(2R)^{p}+R^{p}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\left\lVert f_{2}-g_{2}\right\rVert^{p}_{X^{\infty}_{p,q}}R^{p}\Big\}

(8.53)

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\Big\{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert^{p}_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}\big[(2^{p}+1)R^{p}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\left\lVert f_{2}-g_{2}\right\rVert^{p}_{X^{\infty}_{p,q}}R^{p}\Big\}

\displaystyle\big(K_{p}=C_{p}(2^{p}+1)\big)\quad

\displaystyle\leq K_{p}R^{p}\Big\{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert^{p}_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}+\left\lVert f_{2}-g_{2}\right\rVert^{p}_{X^{\infty}_{p,q}}\Big\}

\displaystyle[a>0,b>0,\ a^{p}+b^{p}\leq(a+b)^{p}]\quad

\displaystyle\leq K_{p}R^{p}\Big\{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}+\left\lVert f_{2}-g_{2}\right\rVert_{X^{\infty}_{p,q}}\Big\}^{p}

(8.54)

using
K_{p}=C_{p}(2^{p}+1)
and
a^{p}+b^{p}\leq(a+b)^{p}
for
a>0,b>0
.
Finally,

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\mathcal{F}}(\boldsymbol{b}_{0},\boldsymbol{f})-{\mathcal{F}}(\boldsymbol{b}_{0},\boldsymbol{g})\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}}

\displaystyle\leq K_{p}^{{}^{1}\!/_{p}}R\Big\{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}_{1}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}+\left\lVert f_{2}-g_{2}\right\rVert_{X^{\infty}_{p,q}}\Big\}

\displaystyle=\rho_{0}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}-\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}}

(8.55)

and
\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}(\boldsymbol{b}_{0},\boldsymbol{f})
is a contraction on the space
\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}
as soon as

\rho_{0}=K_{p}^{{}^{1}\!/_{p}}R<1\quad\text{or}\quad R<\frac{1}{K_{p}^{{}^{1}\!/_{p}}}.

(8.56)

In this case, the map
\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}(\boldsymbol{b}_{0},\boldsymbol{f})
defined in (
8.10
) has a fixed point
\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}\\
h\end{bmatrix}
in
\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}
:

{\mathcal{F}}\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{b}_{0},\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}\\
h\end{bmatrix}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}\\
h\end{bmatrix},\ \text{or}\ \begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}\\
h\end{bmatrix}(t)=e^{\mathbb{A}_{F,q}t}\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\\
h_{0}\end{bmatrix}-\int_{0}^{t}e^{\mathbb{A}_{F,q}(t-\tau)}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathcal{N}}_{q}\boldsymbol{z}(\tau)\\[2.84526pt]
{\mathcal{M}}_{q}[\boldsymbol{z}]h(\tau)\end{bmatrix}d\tau,

(8.57)

and such point
\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}\\
h\end{bmatrix}
is the unique solution of the translated non-linear system (
8.2
), or (
8.3
), with finite dimensional control

\begin{bmatrix}P_{q}m(\boldsymbol{u})\\[5.69054pt]
v\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\displaystyle P_{q}\Bigg(m\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bigg(P_{N}\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}\\
h\end{bmatrix},\boldsymbol{p}_{k}\bigg)\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\bigg)\Bigg)\\[8.53581pt]
\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bigg(P_{N}\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}\\
h\end{bmatrix},\boldsymbol{p}_{k}\bigg)f_{k}\end{bmatrix}

in feedback form, as described by Eq (
8.1
). Theorem
8.3
and hence Theorem
8.1
are proved. ∎

Remark 8.1
.

Recall from (
2.1
) and the statement of Theorem
2.1
, that
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}_{k}\in(\widehat{\boldsymbol{W}}^{q}_{\sigma})^{u}_{N}\subset\widehat{\boldsymbol{L}}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)
as established in Appendix
C
. This means that the feedback control acting on the fluid variable
\boldsymbol{u}
is of reduced dimension
(d-1)
to include the
d
th
component. ∎

\begin{Bmatrix}\text{ If }\left\lVert\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\\
h_{0}\end{bmatrix}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)}\leq r_{1}\\[11.38109pt]
\text{for }r_{1}\text{ sufficiently small}\end{Bmatrix}\implies\begin{Bmatrix}\text{ The solution }[\boldsymbol{z},h]\text{ satisfies}\\[8.53581pt]
\left\lVert\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}\\
h\end{bmatrix}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}}\leq r\end{Bmatrix}

(9.13)

where the constant
r
satisfies the constraint (
8.29
) in terms of the constant
r_{1}
and some constant
C
in (
8.27
) or (
8.28
) or (
8.15
) or (
8.16
). We seek to guarantee that we have

\left\lVert\boldsymbol{z}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}\leq\frac{1}{2C_{3}}<\frac{1}{2C},\mbox{ hence }\frac{1}{2}\leq\Big[1-C_{3}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{z}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}\Big],

(9.14)

where we can always take the constant
C_{3}
in (
9.14
) greater than the constant
C
in (
8.29
), (
8.30
), (
8.31
).Then (
9.14
) can be achieved by choosing
r_{1}>0
sufficiently small. In fact, as
r_{1}\searrow 0
. Eq. (
8.30
) shows that the interval
r_{\min}\leq r\leq r_{\max}
of corresponding values of
r
tends to the interval
[0,\frac{1}{C}]
. Thus, (
9.14
) can be achieved as
r_{\min}\searrow 0
:
0<r_{\min}<r<\frac{1}{2C}
. Next (
9.14
) implies that (
9.12
) becomes

\left\lVert\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}\\
h\end{bmatrix}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}\times X^{\infty}_{p,q}}\leq 2M_{1}\left\lVert\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\\
h_{0}\end{bmatrix}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)}\leq 2M_{1}r_{1}.

(9.15)

by (
9.13
). Substituting (
9.15
) in estimate (
9.9
) then yields

\displaystyle\left\lVert\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}(t)\\
h(t)\end{bmatrix}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq M_{\gamma_{0}}e^{-\gamma_{0}t}\left\lVert\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\\
h_{0}\end{bmatrix}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)}+2M_{1}C_{3}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{z}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{X}^{\infty}_{p,q,\sigma}}\left\lVert\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\\
h_{0}\end{bmatrix}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{M}\Big[e^{-\gamma_{0}t}+4\widehat{M}C_{3}r_{1}\Big]\left\lVert\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\\
h_{0}\end{bmatrix}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)}

(9.16)

again by (
9.15
) with
\displaystyle\widehat{M}=\max\{M_{\gamma_{0}},M_{1}\}
. This is the counterpart of
[
60
, Eq (9.16)]
.

Step 4:
We now take
T
sufficiently large and
r_{1}>0
sufficiently small so that

\beta=\widehat{M}\left[e^{-\gamma_{0}T}+4\widehat{M}C_{3}r_{1}\right]<1.

(9.17)

Then (
9.16
) implies

\left\lVert\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}(T)\\
h(T)\end{bmatrix}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)}\leq\beta\left\lVert\begin{bmatrix}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{0}\\
h_{0}\end{bmatrix}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)}

(9.18)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega)\times B^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega)
, and hence

\left\lVert\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}(nT)\\
h(nT)\end{bmatrix}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)}\leq\beta\left\lVert\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}((n-1)T)\\
h((n-1)T)\end{bmatrix}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)}\leq\beta^{n}\left\lVert\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\\
h_{0}\end{bmatrix}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)}.

(9.19)

Since
\beta<1
, the semigroup property of the evolution implies
[
6
]
that there are constants
M_{\widetilde{\gamma}}\geq 1,\widetilde{\gamma}>0
such that

\left\lVert\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}(t)\\
h(t)\end{bmatrix}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)}\leq M_{\widetilde{\gamma}}e^{-\widetilde{\gamma}t}\left\lVert\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\\
h_{0}\end{bmatrix}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)}

(9.20)

with
\displaystyle\left\lVert[\boldsymbol{z}_{0},h_{0}]\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)}\leq r_{1}=
small. This proves (
2.18
), i.e Theorem
2.3
. ∎

Remark 9.1
.

The above computations - (
9.17
) through (
9.19
) - can be used to support qualitatively the intuitive expectation that “the larger the decay rate
\gamma_{0}
in (
6.7
) = (
9.2
) of the linearized feedback
\boldsymbol{w}
-dynamics (
5.4
), the larger the decay rate
\widetilde{\gamma}
in (
9.20
) of the nonlinear feedback
\{\boldsymbol{z},h\}
-dynamics (
9.1
) or (
2.13
); hence the larger the rate
\widetilde{\gamma}
in (
9.20
) of the original
\{\boldsymbol{y},\theta\}
-dynamics in (
1.1
)”.

The following considerations are somewhat qualitative. Let
S(t)
denote the non-linear semigroup in the space
\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)\equiv\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega)\times B^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega)
, with infinitesimal generator
\displaystyle\big[\mathbb{A}_{{}_{F,q}}-{\mathcal{N}}_{q}\big]
describing the feedback
\{\boldsymbol{z},h\}
-dynamics, (
9.1
) or (
2.13
) as guaranteed by the well posedness Theorem
2.2
= Theorem
8.1
. Thus,
\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}(t)\\
h(t)\end{bmatrix}=S(t)\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{z}_{0}\\
h_{0}\end{bmatrix}
on
\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)
. By (
9.17
), we can rewrite (
9.18
) as:

\left\lVert S(T)\right\rVert_{{\mathcal{L}}\big(\boldsymbol{V}_{b}^{q,p}(\Omega)\big)}\leq\beta<1.

(9.21)

It follows from
[
6
, p 178]
via the semigroup property that

-\widetilde{\gamma}\ \ \text{is just below}\ \ \frac{\ln\beta}{T}<0.

(9.22)

Pick
r_{1}>0
in (
9.17
) so small that
4\widehat{M}^{2}C_{3}r_{1}
is negligible, so that
\beta
is just above
\displaystyle\widehat{M}e^{-\gamma_{0}T}
, so
\ln\beta
is just above
\displaystyle\big[\ln\widehat{M}-\gamma_{0}T\big]
, hence

\frac{\ln\beta}{T}\text{ is just above }\Big[(-\gamma_{0})+\frac{\ln\widehat{M}}{T}\Big].

(9.23)

Hence, by (
9.22
), (
9.23
),

\widetilde{\gamma}\sim\gamma_{0}-\frac{\ln\widehat{M}}{T}

(9.24)

and the larger
\gamma_{0}
, the larger is
\widetilde{\gamma}
, as desired.

A
Some auxiliary results for the Stokes and Oseen operators: analytic semigroup generation, maximal regularity, domains of fractional powers.

In this section we collect some known results used in the paper. As a prerequisite of the present Appendix
A
, we make reference to the paragraph
Definition of Besov spaces
\boldsymbol{B}^{s}_{q,p}
on domains of class
C^{1}
as real interpolation of Sobolev spaces
, Eqts (
1.7
)-(
1.11
) and Remark
1.2
.

(a)

The Stokes and Oseen operators generate a strongly continuous analytic semigroup on
\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)
,
1<q<\infty
.

Theorem A.1
.

Let
d\geq 2,1<q<\infty
and let
\Omega
be a bounded domain in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
of class
C^{3}
. Then

(i)

the Stokes operator
-A_{q}=P_{q}\Delta
in (
1.14
), repeated here as

-A_{q}\psi=P_{q}\Delta\psi,\quad\psi\in\mathcal{D}(A_{q})=\boldsymbol{W}^{2,q}(\Omega)\cap\boldsymbol{W}^{1,q}_{0}(\Omega)\cap\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)

(A.1)

generates a s.c. analytic semigroup
e^{-A_{q}t}
on
\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)
. See
[
33
]
and the review paper
[
37
, Theorem 2.8.5 p 17]
.

(ii)

The Oseen operator
{\mathcal{A}}_{q}
in (
1.17
)

{\mathcal{A}}_{q}=-(\nu A_{q}+A_{o,q}),\quad{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{A}}_{q})={\mathcal{D}}(A_{q})\subset\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)

(A.2)

generates a s.c. analytic semigroup
e^{{\mathcal{A}}_{q}t}
on
\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)
. This follows as
A_{o,q}
is relatively bounded with respect to
A^{{}^{1}\!/_{2}}_{q}
, see (
1.15
): thus a standard theorem on perturbation of an analytic semigroup generator applies
[
68
, Corollary 2.4, p 81]
.

(iii)

\displaystyle 0\in\rho(A_{q})

\displaystyle=\text{ the resolvent set of the Stokes operator }A_{q}

(A.3a)

\displaystyle\begin{picture}\put(-40.0,10.0){ $\left\{\rule{0.0pt}{18.0pt}\right.$}\end{picture}A_{q}^{-1}

\displaystyle:\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\longrightarrow\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\text{ is compact}.

(A.3b)

(iv)

The s.c. analytic Stokes semigroup
e^{-A_{q}t}
is uniformly stable on
\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)
: there exist constants
M\geq 1,\delta>0
(possibly depending on
q
) such that

\left\lVert e^{-A_{q}t}\right\rVert_{{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega))}\leq Me^{-\delta t},\ t>0.

(A.4)

(b)

Domains of fractional powers,
{\mathcal{D}}(A_{q}^{\alpha}),0<\alpha<1
of the Stokes operator
A_{q}
on
\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega),1<q<\infty
.
We elaborate on (
1.16
)

Theorem A.2
.

For the domains of fractional powers
{\mathcal{D}}(A_{q}^{\alpha}),0<\alpha<1
, of the Stokes operator
A_{q}
in (
A.1
) = (
1.14
), the following complex interpolation relation holds true
[
34
]
and
[
37
, Theorem 2.8.5, p 18]

[{\mathcal{D}}(A_{q}),\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)]_{1-\alpha}={\mathcal{D}}(A_{q}^{\alpha}),\ 0<\alpha<1,\ 1<q<\infty;

(A.5)

in particular

[{\mathcal{D}}(A_{q}),\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)]_{\frac{1}{2}}={\mathcal{D}}(A_{q}^{{}^{1}\!/_{2}})\equiv\boldsymbol{W}_{0}^{1,q}(\Omega)\cap\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega).

(A.6)

Thus, on the space
{\mathcal{D}}(A_{q}^{{}^{1}\!/_{2}})
, the norms

\left\lVert\nabla\ \cdot\ \right\rVert_{L^{q}(\Omega)}\text{ and }\left\lVert\ \right\rVert_{L^{q}(\Omega)}

(A.7)

are related via Poincaré inequality.

(c)

The Stokes operator
-A_{q}
and the Oseen operator
{\mathcal{A}}_{q},1<q<\infty
generate s.c. analytic semigroups on the Besov space, from (
1.11
)

\displaystyle\Big(\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega),\mathcal{D}(A_{q})\Big)_{1-\frac{1}{p},p}

\displaystyle=\Big\{\boldsymbol{g}\in\boldsymbol{B}^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega):\text{ div }\boldsymbol{g}=0,\ \boldsymbol{g}|_{\Gamma}=0\Big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\text{if }\frac{1}{q}<2-\frac{2}{p}<2;

(A.8a)

\displaystyle\Big(\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega),\mathcal{D}(A_{q})\Big)_{1-\frac{1}{p},p}

\displaystyle=\Big\{\boldsymbol{g}\in\boldsymbol{B}^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega):\text{ div }\boldsymbol{g}=0,\ \boldsymbol{g}\cdot\nu|_{\Gamma}=0\Big\}

\displaystyle\equiv\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega),\ \text{ if }0<2-\frac{2}{p}<\frac{1}{q}.

(A.8b)

Theorem
A.1
states that the Stokes operator
-A_{q}
generates a s.c. analytic semigroup on the space
\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega),\ 1<q<\infty
, hence on the space
{\mathcal{D}}(A_{q})
in (
1.14
) = (

), with norm

as

. Then, one obtains that the Stokes operator
-A_{q}
generates a s.c. analytic semigroup on the real interpolation spaces in (

as

is bounded on

. Moreover

{\mathcal{A}}_{q}
generates a s.c. analytic semigroup on the real interpolation space of (

1.2
. The objective of the present paper is precisely to obtain stabilization results on spaces that do not recognize B.C.

Theorem A.3
.

Let
1<q<\infty,1<p<{}^{2q}\!/_{2q-1}

(d)

Space of maximal

We return to the dynamic Stokes problem in

The solution of Eq (

\boldsymbol{\varphi}(t)=e^{-A_{q}t}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-A_{q}(t-s)}F_{\sigma}(\tau)d\tau.

The following is the celebrated result on maximal regularity on

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\bigg\{\left\lVert F_{\sigma}\right\rVert_{L^{p}(0,T;\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{0}\right\rVert_{\big(\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega),{\mathcal{D}}(A_{q})\big)_{1-\frac{1}{p},p}}\bigg\}.

(iii)

\displaystyle\equiv 0

\displaystyle\text{ in }\Omega,

(A.22d)

L_{e}(\boldsymbol{\varphi})=(\boldsymbol{y}_{e}.\nabla)\boldsymbol{\varphi}+(\boldsymbol{\varphi}.\nabla)\boldsymbol{y}_{e}\hskip 85.35826pt

(A.23)

rewritten in abstract form, after applying the Helmholtz projector
P_{q}
to (
A.22a
) and recalling
{\mathcal{A}}_{q}
in (
A.2
), as

\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{t}={\mathcal{A}}_{q}\boldsymbol{\varphi}+P_{q}\boldsymbol{F}=-\nu A_{q}\boldsymbol{\varphi}-A_{o,q}\boldsymbol{\varphi}+\boldsymbol{F}_{\sigma},\quad\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{0}\in\big(\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega),{\mathcal{D}}(A_{q})\big)_{1-\frac{1}{p},p}

(A.24)

whose solution is via (
1.18
) = (
A.2
)

\boldsymbol{\varphi}(t)=e^{{\mathcal{A}}_{q}t}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{{\mathcal{A}}_{q}(t-\tau)}\boldsymbol{F}_{\sigma}(\tau)d\tau,

(A.25)

\boldsymbol{\varphi}(t)=e^{-\nu A_{q}t}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\nu A_{q}(t-\tau)}\boldsymbol{F}_{\sigma}(\tau)d\tau-\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\nu A_{q}(t-\tau)}A_{o,q}\boldsymbol{\varphi}(\tau)d\tau.

(A.26)

Theorem A.5
.

Let
1<p,q<\infty,\ 0<T<\infty
. Assume (as in (
A.16
))

\boldsymbol{F}_{\sigma}\in L^{p}\big(0,T;\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)\big),\quad\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{0}\in\big(\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega),{\mathcal{D}}(A_{q})\big)_{1-\frac{1}{p},p}

(A.27)

where
{\mathcal{D}}(A_{q})={\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{A}}_{q})
, see (
A.2
) = (
1.18
). Then there exists a unique solution
\boldsymbol{\varphi}\in\boldsymbol{\widetilde{X}}^{T}_{p,q,\sigma},\ \pi\in\widetilde{Y}^{T}_{p,q}
of the dynamic Oseen problem (
A.22
), continuously on the data: that is, there exist constants
C_{0},C_{1}
independent of
\boldsymbol{F}_{\sigma},\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{0}
such that

\displaystyle C_{0}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C\big([0,T];\boldsymbol{B}^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega)\big)}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\varphi}\right\rVert_{\boldsymbol{\widetilde{X}}^{T}_{p,q,\sigma}}+\left\lVert\pi\right\rVert_{\widetilde{Y}^{T}_{p,q}}

\displaystyle\equiv\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\varphi}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{L^{p}(0,T;\boldsymbol{L}^{q}(\Omega))}+\left\lVert A_{q}\boldsymbol{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{p}(0,T;\boldsymbol{L}^{q}(\Omega))}+\left\lVert\pi\right\rVert_{\widetilde{Y}^{T}_{p,q}}

(A.28)

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\left\{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{F}_{\sigma}\right\rVert_{L^{p}(0,T;\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega))}+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{0}\right\rVert_{\big(\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega),{\mathcal{D}}(A_{q})\big)_{1-\frac{1}{p},p}}\right\}

(A.29)

where
T<\infty
. Equivalently, for
1<p,q<\infty

i.

The map

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{F}_{\sigma}\longrightarrow\int_{0}^{t}e^{{\mathcal{A}}_{q}(t-\tau)}\boldsymbol{F}_{\sigma}(\tau)d\tau\ :\text{continuous}

(A.30)

\displaystyle L^{p}(0,T;\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow L^{p}\big(0,T;{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{A}}_{q})={\mathcal{D}}(A_{q})\big)

where then automatically, see (
A.24
)

L^{p}(0,T;\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega))\longrightarrow W^{1,p}(0,T;\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega))

(A.31)

and ultimately via (
A.12
)

L^{p}(0,T;\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega))\longrightarrow\boldsymbol{\widetilde{X}}^{T}_{p,q,\sigma}\equiv L^{p}\big(0,T;{\mathcal{D}}(A_{q})\big)\cap W^{1,p}(0,T;\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega)).

(A.32)

ii.

The s.c. analytic semigroup
e^{{\mathcal{A}}_{q}t}
generated by the Oseen operator
{\mathcal{A}}_{q}
(see (
A.2
) = (
1.18
)) on the space
\displaystyle\big(\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega),{\mathcal{D}}(A_{q})\big)_{1-\frac{1}{p},p}
satisfies for
1<p,q<\infty

e^{{\mathcal{A}}_{q}t}:\ \text{continuous}\quad\big(\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega),{\mathcal{D}}(A_{q})\big)_{1-\frac{1}{p},p}\longrightarrow L^{p}\big(0,T;{\mathcal{D}}({\mathcal{A}}_{q})={\mathcal{D}}(A_{q})\big)

(A.33)

and hence automatically by (
A.12
)

e^{{\mathcal{A}}_{q}t}:\ \text{continuous}\quad\big(\boldsymbol{L}^{q}_{\sigma}(\Omega),{\mathcal{D}}(A_{q})\big)_{1-\frac{1}{p},p}\longrightarrow\boldsymbol{\widetilde{X}}^{T}_{p,q,\sigma}.

(A.34)

In particular, for future use, for
1<q<\infty,1<p<\frac{2q}{2q-1}
, we have that the s.c. analytic semigroup
\displaystyle e^{{\mathcal{A}}_{q}t}
on the space
\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega)
, satisfies

(A.35)

and hence automatically

e^{{\mathcal{A}}_{q}t}:\ \text{continuous}\quad\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}^{2-{}^{2}\!/_{p}}_{q,p}(\Omega)\longrightarrow\boldsymbol{\widetilde{X}}^{T}_{p,q,\sigma},\ T<\infty.

(A.36)
